Let's write:\n\n```\n__global__ void panelFactor(double* A, int n, int k, int b, int* failFlag) {\n    int tid = threadIdx.x;\n    // loop over columns\n    for (int j = 0; j < b; ++j) {\n        if (tid == 0) {\n            double sum = 0;\n            for (int p=0;p<j;++p){ double v=A[(k+j)*n + k+p]; sum += v*v; }\n            double val = A[(k+j)*n + k+j] - sum;\n            if (val <= 0) { atomicMin(failFlag, k+j); A[(k+j)*n+k+j] = 0.0; }\n            else A[(k+j)*n+k+j] = sqrt(val);\n        }\n        __syncthreads();\n        if (tid > j) {\n            int i = tid;\n            double sum=0;\n            for (int p=0;p<j;++p) sum += A[(k+i)*n+k+p]*A[(k+j)*n+k+p];\n            A[(k+i)*n + k+j] = (A[(k+i)*n+k+j] - sum) / A[(k+j)*n+k+j];\n        }\n        __syncthreads(); // needed before next j uses updated column j\n    }\n}\n```\nActually sync after diagonal before using diag; and sync after offdiag updates before next j's diagonal sum (which reads column p values of row j... for next j+1 diagonal reads A[(k+j+1)*n+k+p] for p<=j, including column j values for row j+1 computed by thread j+1). Not needed.\n\nTRSM kernel:\n```\n__global__ void choleskyTrsmKernel(double* A, int n, int k, int b, int m) {\n  __shared__ double L[kBlock][kBlock+1]; // static 33KB, but only if kBlock constant\n  int tid = threadIdx.x;\n  for (int idx = tid; idx < b*b; idx += blockDim.x) {\n    int r = idx / b, c = idx - r*b;\n    L[r][c] = A[(size_t)(k+r)*n + k + c];\n  }\n  __syncthreads();\n  int row = blockIdx.x*blockDim.x + tid;\n  if (row >= m) return; // but all threads participated in loading; return after sync is fine\n  double* a = A + (size_t)(k+b+row)*n + k;\n  for (int j=0;j<b;++j) {\n    double sum = 0;\n    for (int p=0;p<j;++p) sum += a[p]*L[j][p];\n    a[j] = (a[j]-sum)/L[j][j];\n  }\n}\n```\nBut `__shared__ double L[64][65]` static = 33.28KB; plus panel kernel's shared? Good.\n\nPanel kernel uses no shared.\n\nGEMM kernel:\n```\n__global__ void choleskyGemmKernel(double* A, int n, int k, int b, int m) {\n  const int tileRow = blockIdx.x, tileCol = blockIdx.y;\n  if (tileCol > tileRow) return;\n  const int row0 = k + b + tileRow*kTile;\n  const int col0 = k + b + tileCol*kTile;\n  const int tx = threadIdx.x; // 0..15\n  const int ty = threadIdx.y; // 0..15\n  __shared__ double As[kTile][kTileK+1];\n  __shared__ double Bs[kTile][kTileK+1];\n  double acc[kMicro][kMicro] = {};\n  for (int kk=0; kk<b; kk+=kTileK) {\n     // load\n     int klocal = min(kTileK, b-kk);\n     for (int idx = ty*blockDim.x + tx; idx < kTile*klocal; idx += blockDim.x*blockDim.y) {\n        int r = idx / klocal;\n        int c = idx - r*klocal;\n        int gr = row0 + r;\n        int gr2 = col0 + r;\n        As[r][c] = (gr < n) ? A[(size_t)gr*n + k + kk + c] : 0.0;\n        Bs[r][c] = (gr2 < n) ? A[(size_t)gr2*n + k + kk + c] : 0.0;\n     }\n     __syncthreads();\n     for (int p=0;p<klocal;++p) {\n        double a0=As[ty*kMicro+0][p], a1=..., a2, a3;\n        double b0=Bs[tx*kMicro+0][p], ...\n        acc[0][0] += a0*b0; ... 16 terms\n     }\n     __syncthreads();\n  }\n  // store\n  for i in 0..3: int gr = row0 + ty*kMicro + i; if (gr<n) {\n     double* p = A + (size_t)gr*n + col0 + tx*kMicro;\n     for j: int gc = col0+tx*kMicro+j; if (gc<n) p[j] -= acc[i][j]; // note p points to col0+tx*4; p[j] index okay if gc<n\n  }\n}\n```\nCareful: if col0 + tx*kMicro may exceed n, pointer arithmetic out of bounds even if not dereferenced. Better compute index individually: `int gc = col0+tx*kMicro+j; if (gr<n && gc<n) A[(size_t)gr*n+gc] -= acc[i][j];`.\n\nLoading with `klocal` may vary per chunk; using idx<kTile*klocal and r=idx/klocal. j < b; ++j) {\n        if (tid == 0) {\n            double sum = 0.0;\n            for (int p = 0; p < j; ++p) {\n                const double v = A[(size_t)(k + j) * n + k + p];\n                sum += v * v;\n            }\n            const double val = A[(size_t)(k + j) * n + k + j] - sum;\n            if (val <= 0.0) {\n                atomicMin(failFlag, k + j + 1);\n                A[(size_t)(k + j) * n + k + j] = 0.0;\n            } else {\n                A[(size_t)(k + j) * n + k + j] = sqrt(val);\n            }\n        }\n        __syncthreads();\n\n        if (tid > j && tid < b) {\n            const int i = tid;\n            double sum = 0.0;\n            for (int p = 0; p < j; ++p) {\n                sum += A[(size_t)(k + i) * n + k + p] * A[(size_t)(k + j) * n + k + p];\n            }\n            A[(size_t)(k + i) * n + k + j] =\n                (A[(size_t)(k + i) * n + k + j] - sum) / A[(size_t)(k + j) * n + k + j];\n        }\n        __syncthreads();\n    }\n}\n\n__global__ void choleskyTrsmKernel(double* A, int n, int k, int b, int m) {\n    __shared__ double L[kBlock][kBlock + 1];\n\n    const int tid = threadIdx.x;\n    for (int idx = tid; idx < b * b; idx += blockDim.x) {\n        const int r = idx / b;\n        const int c = idx - r * b;\n        L[r][c] = A[(size_t)(k + r) * n + k + c];\n    }\n    __syncthreads();\n\n    const int row = blockIdx.x * blockDim.x + tid;\n    if (row >= m) {\n        return;\n    }\n\n    double* a = A + (size_t)(k + b + row) * n + k;\n    for (int j = 0; j < b; ++j) {\n        double sum = 0.0;\n        for (int p = 0; p < j; ++p) {\n            sum += a[p] * L[j][p];\n        }\n        a[j] = (a[j] - sum) / L[j][j];\n    }\n}\n\n__global__ void choleskyGemmKernel(double* A, int n, int k, int b, int m) {\n    const int tileRow = blockIdx.x;\n    const int tileCol = blockIdx.y;\n    if (tileCol > tileRow) {\n        return;\n    }\n\n    const int row0 = k + b + tileRow * kTile;\n    const int col0 = k + b + tileCol * kTile;\n\n    const int tx = threadIdx.x;\n    const int ty = threadIdx.y;\n\n    __shared__ double As[kTile][kTileK + 1];\n    __shared__ double Bs[kTile][kTileK + 1];\n\n    double acc[kMicro][kMicro] = {};\n\n    for (int kk = 0; kk < b; kk += kTileK) {\n        const int klocal = min(kTileK, b - kk);\n\n        for (int idx = ty * blockDim.x + tx; idx < kTile * klocal;\n             idx += blockDim.x * blockDim.y) {\n            const int r = idx / klocal;\n            const int c = idx - r * klocal;\n            const int gr = row0 + r;\n            const int gc = col0 + r;\n            As[r][c] = (gr < n) ? j < kMicro; ++j) {\n                    acc[i][j] += av[i] * bv[j];\n                }\n            }\n        }\n        __syncthreads();\n    }\n\n#pragma unroll\n    for (int i = 0; i < kMicro; ++i) {\n        const int gr = row0 + ty * kMicro + i;\n        if (gr < n) {\n#pragma unroll\n            for (int j = 0; i < n * n; ++i) {\n        B[i] = (rand_r(&seed) / (double)RAND_MAX) - 0.5;\n    }\n\n    // Compute A = B * B^T\n    for (size_t i = 0; i < n; ++i) {\n        for (size_t j = 0; j < n; ++j) {\n            double sum = 0.0;\n            for (size_t k = 0; k < n; ++k) {\n                sum += B[i * n + k] * B[j * n + k];\n            }\n            A[i * n + j] = sum;\n        }\n    }\n\n    // Add diagonal dominance to ensure positive definiteness\n    for (size_t i = 0; i < n; ++i) {\n        A[i * n + i] += n;\n    }\n}\n\nbool validateCholesky(const std::vector<double>& L, const std::vector<double>& A_orig, const size_t n) {\n    // Validate by computing L * L^T and comparing with original matrix\n\n    std::vector<double> reconstructed(n * n);\n\n    // Compute L * L^T\n    for (size_t i = 0; Use blockIdx linear scheduling with an algorithm:\n```\nint t = blockIdx.x;\nwhile (t < T) {\n   // find row: r = floor((sqrt(8t+1)-1)/2)\n   int r = (int)((sqrt(8.0*t+1.0)-1.0)*0.5);\n   while ((r+1)*(r+2)/2 <= t) ++r;\n   while (r*(r+1)/2 > t) --r;\n   int c = t - r*(r+1)/2;\n   // compute tile (row=r, col=c)\n   t += gridDim.x;\n}\n```\nGood.\n\nAlternatively launch GEMM as a separate kernel but with fewer launches overall? Let's verify with a small test later.\n\nAlternatively, use a \"single kernel with spin-lock global barrier\" not requiring cooperative launch, but grid must still be resident. Cooperative is cleaner.\n\nImplementation of persistent kernel:\n\n```\n__global__ void choleskyPersistentKernel(double* A, int n, int* failFlag) {\n    extern __shared__ double smem[];\n    double* As = smem;                       // kTile*(kTileK+1)\n    double* Bs = smem + kTile*(kTileK+1);\n    double* Lp = smem;                       // alias, kBlock*(kBlock+1)\n\n    for (int k = 0; k < n; k += kBlock) {\n        int b = min(kBlock, n-k);\n        // ---- panel factor by block 0 ----\n        if (blockIdx.x == 0) {\n            // load block into Lp\n            for (int idx = threadIdx.x; idx < b*b; idx += blockDim.x) {\n                int r = idx / b, c = idx - r*b;\n                Lp[r*(kBlock+1)+c] = A[(size_t)(k+r)*n + k + c];\n            }\n            __syncthreads();\n            for (int j=0; j<b; ++j) {\n                if (threadIdx.x == 0) { diag ... using Lp }\n                __syncthreads();\n                int i = threadIdx.x;\n                if (i > j && i < b) { offdiag using Lp }\n                __syncthreads();\n            }\n            // write back lower block (including diag) to global\n            for (int idx=threadIdx.x; idx<b*b; idx+=blockDim.x) {\n                int r = idx/b, c = idx-r*b;\n                if (c <= r) A[(size_t)(k+r)*n + k+c] = Lp[r*(kBlock+1)+c];\n            }\n            // Note: no trailing __syncthreads needed before grid sync? grid.sync includes block sync? cooperative grid sync synchronizes all threads; yes grid.sync() acts as barrier across all threads. But we need all threads in block 0 have finished writes; grid.sync ensures. Fine.\n        }\n        grid.sync();\n\n        int m = n - k - b;\n        // ---- TRSM distributed ----\n        if (m > 0) {\n            int total = gridDim.x * blockDim.x;\n            for (int row = blockIdx.x*blockDim.x + threadIdx.x; row < m; row += total) {\n                double* a = A + (size_t)(k+b+row)*n + k;\n                for (int j=0;j<b;++j) {\n                    double sum=0;\n                    for (int p=0;p<j;++p) sum += a[p]*A[(size_t)(k+j)*n + k+p];\n                    a[j] = (a[j]-sum)/A[(size_t)(k+j)*n+k+j];\n                }\n            }\n        }\n        grid.sync();\n\n        // ---- GEMM distributed ----\n        if (m > 0) {\n            int ntiles = (m + kTile - 1)/kTile;\n            long long T = (long long)ntiles*(ntiles+1)/2;\n            for (long long t = blockIdx.x; t < T; t += gridDim.x) {\n                // map\n                int r = (int)((sqrt(8.0*(double)t + 1.0) - 1.0) * 0.5);\n                while ((long long)(r+1)*(r+2)/2 <= t) ++r;\n                while ((long long)r*(r+1)/2 > t) --r;\n                int c = (int)(t - (long long)r*(r+1)/2);\n                // gemm tile (r,c)\n                gemmTile(A, n, k, b, m, r, c, As, Bs);\n                __syncthreads(); // within block before reusing shared for next tile\n            }\n        }\n        grid.sync();\n    }\n    // zero upper\n    ...\n}\n```\n\nWait: GEMM tile function uses shared As/Bs and __syncthreads internally. Since all threads in block participate in each tile, fine. After finishing a tile, before starting next tile, need __syncthreads to ensure no thread starts overwriting shared while others still reading? The store phase happens after the K loop and last __syncthreads; after stores, threads proceed to next tile and start loading As/Bs. Other threads may still be in store phase but store reads only acc registers and global, not shared. Loading new As/Bs could overwrite shared that another thread uses? After the last K loop's __syncthreads, all threads finished reading shared for compute. Stores don't use shared. So next tile's loads are safe even without sync? But the current gemm function starts a new K loop loading shared; a thread that finished stores early may load shared while another thread is still in the store loop (not using shared). Safe. But also next tile's compute would need __syncthreads after loads; the function does loads then __syncthreads, but a thread could reach that sync while another is still in the previous tile's store loop (not in sync), causing deadlock? __syncthreads is block-wide; all threads must reach the same barrier. Thread A finished stores, goes to next tile, loads, reaches __syncthreads (the first one of the tile). Thread B still in previous tile store, then loads, then reaches same barrier. That's fine—both eventually reach; barrier just waits. No incorrect shared data because all loads complete before the barrier and all previous reads completed before the previous last barrier. So it's safe, though a __syncthreads at the end of each tile is clearer. But deadlock risk if some threads exit the loop (different t iterations) while others continue? All threads in a block iterate the same t sequence (t starts blockIdx.x, stride gridDim.x), same number of iterations. So all enter/exit together. Good. But still ensure the first tile starts after panel shared reuse: panel phase block 0 used Lp aliased with As/Bs. grid.sync after panel ensures all block 0 threads finished writing back. Other blocks reach grid.sync and wait.\n\nPanel factor block 0 with 256 threads (grid block size 256) using only threads < b for offdiag. That's fine, but has __syncthreads for all 256. Diagonal thread 0 serial. To speed up panel, we can use more threads to parallelize diagonal sum? Fine.\n\nHowever, note: panel factor loading b*b from global into shared: coalescing? idx mapping r=idx/b, c=idx-r*b; consecutive idx -> consecutive c, coalesced within rows. Good, 4096 elements, 256 threads, 16 iterations.\n\nWriteback lower only, coalesced similarly.\n\nTRSM: Reading L11 from global each inner loop. Could also use shared but shared is used by GEMM alias; during TRSM we can stage L11 into the same shared buffer (block-wide) once per block. Each block loads L11 (b*b) into shared Lp, then all its rows use it. Cost: grid blocks * 32KB global reads = 28 blocks * 32KB = 0.9MB for n=512; 164*32KB = 5MB for n=4096, from L2 (L11 is 32KB and just written; likely L2). That's cheap. Shared loads are fast and no global broadcast latency. Then TRSM inner loop uses shared Lp. Let's do that: \n```\nfor idx=tid; idx<b*b; idx+=blockDim.x: Lp[...] = A[...]\n__syncthreads();\nrows loop ...\n__syncthreads()? before GEMM reuses shared. Since GEMM loads shared after; a thread that finished rows could start loading As (same shared) while another thread still reading Lp in TRSM -> race! Need __syncthreads after TRSM before GEMM's shared use. In previous standalone kernel used dim3(16,16); same linear layout: threadIdx.x 0..15, threadIdx.y 0..15, linear tid = tx + ty*16. So tx = tid & 15, ty = tid >> 4.\n\nLoading:\n```\nfor (int idx = tid; idx < kTile*klocal; idx += kThreads) {\n   int r = idx / klocal, c = idx - r*klocal;\n   ...\n}\n```\nUsing kThreads constant 256.\n\nCompute:\n```\nfor p...\n  av[i]=As[(ty*kMicro+i)*(kTileK+1)+p]; // panel use\n    const int tid = threadIdx.x;\n\n    for (int k = 0; k < n; k += kBlock) {\n        const int b = min(kBlock, n - k);\n        const int m = n - k - b;\n\n        if (blockIdx.x == 0) {\n            for (int idx = tid; idx < b*b; idx += kThreads) {\n                int r = idx / b, c = idx - r*b;\n                Lp[r*(kBlock+1)+c] = A[(size_t)(k+r)*n + k + c];\n            }\n            __syncthreads();\n            for (int j=0;j<b;++j) {\n                if (tid == 0) {\n                    double sum=0;\n                    for (int p=0;p<j;++p) { double v=Lp[j*(kBlock+1)+p]; sum+=v*v; }\n                    double val = Lp[j*(kBlock+1)+j] - sum;\n                    if (val <= 0) { atomicMin(failFlag, k+j+1); Lp[j*(kBlock+1)+j]=0; }\n                    else Lp[j*(kBlock+1)+j] = sqrt(val);\n                }\n                __syncthreads();\n                if (tid > j && tid < b) {\n                    int i = tid;\n                    double sum=0;\n                    for (int p=0;p<j;++p) sum += Lp[i*(kBlock+1)+p]*Lp[j*(kBlock+1)+p];\n                    Lp[i*(kBlock+1)+j] = (Lp[i*(kBlock+1)+j]-sum)/Lp[j*(kBlock+1)+j];\n                }\n                __syncthreads();\n            }\n            for (int idx = tid; idx < b*b; idx += kThreads) {\n                int r = idx / b, c = idx - r*b;\n                if (c <= r) A[(size_t)(k+r)*n + k + c] = Lp[r*(kBlock+1)+c];\n            }\n        }\n        grid.sync();\n\n        if (m > 0) {\n            // stage L11 to shared for this block\n            for (int idx = tid; idx < b*b; idx += kThreads) {\n                int r = idx / b, c = idx - r*b;\n                Lp[r*(kBlock+1)+c] = A[(size_t)(k+r)*n + k + c];\n            }\n            __syncthreads();\n            const int totalThreads = gridDim.x * blockDim.x;\n            for (int row = blockIdx.x*blockDim.x + tid; In the `if (m>0)` sections, all blocks call grid.sync regardless (it's outside the if). Good. Panel `if (blockIdx.x==0)` contains __syncthreads only in block 0; other blocks skip to grid.sync (which is a barrier). That's fine because __syncthreads is block-local, and grid.sync is thread-level across all threads; non-block-0 threads wait. However, there's a subtlety: block 0 executes __syncthreads inside the panel loop; all threads of block 0 participate. Thread j accumulated those in prior iterations. Perfect.\n  \nThis removes the serial thread-0 loop. Implementation:\n\n```\ndouble sumSq = 0.0;\nfor (int j=0;j<b;++j) {\n    if (tid == j) {\n        double val = Lp[j*(kBlock+1)+j] - sumSq;\n        if (val <= 0) {...} else Lp[..j] = sqrt(val);\n    }\n    __syncthreads();\n    if (tid > j && tid < b) {\n        int i = tid;\n        double sum=0;\n        for (p=0;p<j;++p) sum += Lp[i][p]*Lp[j][p];\n        double v = (Lp[i][j]-sum)/Lp[j][j];\n        Lp[i][j] = v;\n        sumSq += v*v;\n    }\n    __syncthreads();\n}\n```\nWait: thread i accumulates sumSq only for its own row i, but the offdiag loop only runs if tid>j && tid<b. Fix:\n\n```\nload Lp\n__syncthreads();\nif (tid < b) dinv[tid] = 1.0/Lp[...];\n__syncthreads();\n```\n\nYes! That explains non-determinism. Maybe we can intentionally make the algorithm produce bitwise identical results to the sequential original while still parallel? Consider: the original algorithm's results can be reproduced exactly if, for each element, the sum over k is accumulated in the same order and all intermediate L values are bitwise identical. In blocked Cholesky, the trailing update subtracts contributions from earlier panels into A elements before they are used in later sums. The original computes each element as sum over all k at the time of computation. Mathematically same but rounding differs because in blocked, the partial subtraction happens at different times. To be bitwise identical, we'd have to compute each L element using the original order of operations: for element A[i,j], sum_{k<j} L[i,k]*L[j,k] accumulated sequentially. That's exactly an \"unblocked\" column/row algorithm. Can we parallelize that on GPU while preserving per-element sequential accumulation order? Yes! The original algorithm processes rows i sequentially and within row columns j sequentially, but each element's sum is independent given prior rows/columns. Actually for row i, element (i,j) depends on row j values (already computed) and row i values for k<j. Within row i, elements j=0..i-1 can be computed in parallel? For off-diagonal element (i,j), sum uses A[i,k] for k<j, which are row i's own previously computed L values. So within a row, j must be sequential. But across rows i, there is dependency: element (i,j) uses L[j,k] from row j; rows can be processed in increasing order, and within each row columns sequential. So the original is inherently sequential row by row, but could parallelize across rows? Row i uses rows j<i, so all rows depend on all previous rows. No parallelism except within a row's dot product. So a bitwise-exact GPU version would be nearly serial (one thread per row, but rows sequential due to dependencies) → not performant. Not acceptable.\n\nThus tolerance is the only sensible expectation. gridDim.x) {\n        for (int j = i + 1 + tid; j < n; j += blockDim.x) {\n            A[(size_t)i * n + j] = 0.0;\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n}  // namespace\n\n// Blocked Cholesky decomposition on the GPU:\n// A = L * L^T, with L written to the lower triangle of A (row-major).\nbool choleskyDecomposition(std::vector<double>& A, const size_t n,\n                           double* d_A, int* d_failFlag) {\n    if (n == 0) {\n        return true;\n    }\n\n    int ni = static_cast<int>(n);\n    const size_t bytes = n * n * sizeof(double);\n\n    cudaMemcpy(d_A, A.data(), bytes, cudaMemcpyHostToDevice);\n    cudaMemset(d_failFlag, 0, sizeof(int));\n\n    int device = 0;\n    cudaGetDevice(&device);\n    int smCount = 0;\n    cudaDeviceGetAttribute(&smCount, cudaDevAttrMultiProcessorCount, device);\n    int maxBlocksPerSm = 0;\n    cudaOccupancyMaxActiveBlocksPerMultiprocessor(\n        &maxBlocksPerSm, choleskyKernel, kThreads, kSmemDoubles * sizeof(double));\n\n    int maxGrid = maxBlocksPerSm * smCount;\n    if (maxGrid < 1) {\n        maxGrid = 1;\n    }\n\n    const int firstM = ni - std::min(kBlock, ni);\n    int grid = maxGrid;\n    if (firstM > 0) {\n        const int ntiles = (firstM + kTile - 1) / kTile;\n        const long long totalTiles = (long long)ntiles * (ntiles + 1) / 2;\n        if (totalTiles < grid) {\n            grid = static_cast<int>(totalTiles);\n        }\n    } else {\n        grid = 1;\n    }\n    if (grid < 1) {\n        grid = 1;\n    }\n\n    void* args[] = {&d_A, &ni, &d_failFlag};\n    const cudaError_t launchStatus =\n        cudaLaunchCooperativeKernel(reinterpret_cast<void*>(choleskyKernel), dim3(grid),\n                                    dim3(kThreads), args, kSmemDoubles * sizeof(double), 0);\n    if (launchStatus != cudaSuccess) {\n        printf(\"Error: CUDA kernel launch failed: %s\\n\", cudaGetErrorString(launchStatus));\n        return false;\n    }\n\n    int hFail = 0;\n    cudaMemcpy(&hFail, d_failFlag, sizeof(int), cudaMemcpyDeviceToHost);\n    if (hFail != 0) {\n        printf(\"Error: Matrix is not positive definite at diagonal element %d\\n\", hFail - 1);\n        return false;\n    }\n\n    cudaMemcpy(A.data(), d_A, bytes, cudaMemcpyDeviceToHost);\n\n    return true;\n}\n\n// Generate a symmetric positive definite matrix\nvoid generatePositiveDefiniteMatrix(std::vector<double>& A, const size_t n) {\n    // Method: Create A = B * B^T where B is random\n    // This guarantees A is positive semi-definite\n    // Then add identity to make it strictly positive definite\n\n    std::vector<double> B(n * n);\n    unsigned int seed = 42;\n\n    // Generate random matrix B\n    for (size_t i = 0; i < n * n; ++i) {\n        B[i] = (rand_r(&seed) / (double)RAND_MAX) - 0.5;\n    }\n\n    // Compute A = B * B^T\n    for (size_t i = 0; i < n; ++i) {\n        for (size_t j = 0; j < n; ++j) {\n            double sum = 0.0;\n            for (size_t k = 0; k < n; ++k) {\n                sum += B[i * n + k] * B[j * n + k];\n            }\n            A[i * n + j] = sum;\n        }\n    }\n\n    // Add diagonal dominance to ensure positive definiteness\n    for (size_t i = 0; i < n; ++i) {\n        A[i * n + i] += n;\n    }\n}\n\nbool validateCholesky(const std::vector<double>& L, const std::vector<double>& A_orig, const size_t n) {\n    // Validate by computing L * L^T and comparing with original matrix\n\n    std::vector<double> reconstructed(n * n);\n\n    // Compute L * L^T\n    for (size_t i = 0; i < n; ++i) {\n        for (size_t j = 0; j < n; ++j) {\n            double sum = 0.0;\n            for (size_t k = 0; k < n; ++k) {\n                sum += L[i * n + k] * L[j * n + k];\n            }\n            reconstructed[i * n + j] = sum;\n        }\n    }\n\n    // Compare with original\n    double maxError = 0.0;\n    double relError = 0.0;\n\n    for (size_t i = 0; i < n * n; ++i) {\n        const double error = fabs(reconstructed[i] - A_orig[i]);\n        maxError = std::max(maxError, error);\n\n        const double rel = error / (fabs(A_orig[i]) + 1e-10);\n        relError = std::max(relError, rel);\n    }\n\n    printf(\"Max absolute error: %.10e\\n\", maxError);\n    printf(\"Max relative error: %.10e\\n\", relError);\n\n    // Check if error is within tolerance\n    if (relError > 1e-6) {\n        printf(\"Validation failed: relative error too large\\n\");\n        return false;\n    }\n\n    return true;\n}\n\nvoid printUsage(const char* progName) {\n
